k}}}\eta_{\alpha\gamma}(2\eta_{\omega w}\check{L}^{{{m}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.30226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.07304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}\check{X}^{{{wk}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.39357pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.39357pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.34505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.53217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}-\check{L}^{{{m}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.00136pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.00136pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.15945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.54248pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.30226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.07304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{(\omega j)}}}\check{X}^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}).

Consequently, the error associated with the
m
-th coordinate trial of
x
is a linear combination of the cube of the previous errors:
\Delta\lambda^{\rho}\Delta\lambda^{\beta}\Delta\phi^{\omega\sigma}
,
\Delta\lambda^{\rho}\Delta\phi^{\alpha\beta}\Delta\phi^{\omega\sigma}
and
\Delta\phi^{\gamma\rho}\Delta\phi^{\alpha\beta}\Delta\phi^{\omega\sigma}
, as computed by Eq. (
57
), assuming that
H
at
y
is non-singular.
It is noted that the error is not a linear combination of
\Delta\lambda^{\rho}\Delta\lambda^{\beta}\Delta\lambda^{\sigma}
, which implies that once the
\phi^{ui}
’s are known, the corresponding
\lambda^{i}
’s will immediately be known in the next iteration.
This underscores the significance of using Halley’s method as the root-finding algorithm for Eq. (
9
), as it can significantly accelerate the solution process when the eigenvector components,
\phi^{ui}
’s, are nearly known.

7.
Computational cost of proposed method

In this section, we present the computational cost of Halley’s method by evaluating its time complexity per iteration using Eq. (
30
).
Given
\boldsymbol{K}
,
\boldsymbol{I}
and
\boldsymbol{\eta}
, the goal of Halley’s scheme is to calculate the next trial solution
\mathbf{x}
, based on the current trial solution
\hat{\mathbf{x}}
:

(59)

x^{m}=\hat{x}^{m}+(-1)\sum_{n=1}^{Q}\hat{L}^{{{m}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.30226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.07304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{n}}}\hat{f}^{n}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\mathbf{x}=\hat{\mathbf{x}}+(-1)\hat{\mathbf{L}}\hat{\mathbf{f}},

where
\hat{f}^{n}=f^{n}(\hat{x})
, and
m\in\{1,2,\ldots,Q\}
.
The equation on the right-hand side is the matrix representation of the one on the left-hand side.
Hence,
\hat{\mathbf{x}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{f}}
are vectors of size
Q
, and
\hat{\mathbf{L}}
is a square matrix of size
Q
.

Now, if
\hat{\mathbf{x}}
,
\hat{\mathbf{L}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{f}}
are known, the time complexity of
\mathbf{x}
is
\mathrm{tc}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})=\mathcal{O}(Q^{2})=\mathcal{O}(R^{2}P^{2})
.
This follows from the fact that
Q=(R+1)(P+1)
, and that the total number of operations required to calculate
\mathbf{x}
is

(60)

\mathrm{no}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})=Q(2Q+1)=2Q^{2}+Q.

The primes in
\mathrm{tc}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})
and
\mathrm{no}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})
indicate that the corresponding expressions are valid under the assumption that
\hat{\mathbf{x}}
,
\hat{\mathbf{L}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{f}}
have been precomputed.
To eliminate this assumption, and compute the time complexity of
\mathbf{x}
appropriately, it is necessary to compute firstly the matrices
\hat{\mathbf{F}}
,
\hat{\mathbf{X}}
,
\hat{\mathbf{H}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{L}}
and the vector
\hat{\mathbf{f}}
.
This is done below.

Starting with the time complexities of
\hat{\mathbf{F}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{X}}
(
=\hat{\mathbf{F}}^{-1}
), we refer to Eq. (
33
) to observe that:

(61a)

\hat{F}^{{{ui}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49315pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}=(-1)\sum_{j=0}^{P}I^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.24797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.24797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0699pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.62135pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta j}}}\hat{\phi}^{uj}\quad\text{implies}\quad 2(P+1)\text{ operations}.

(61b)

\hat{F}^{{{u}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}=K^{{{u}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}+(-1)\delta^{u}_{\alpha}\sum_{j=0}^{P}I^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.24797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.24797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0699pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.62135pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta j}}}\hat{\lambda}^{j}\quad\text{implies}\quad 2(P+2)\text{ operations}.

(61c)

\hat{F}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}=0\quad\text{implies}\quad 0\text{ operations}.

(61d)

\hat{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}=(-2)\sum_{u=1}^{R}\eta_{\alpha u}\hat{F}^{{{ui}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49315pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}\quad\text{implies}\quad 2R\text{ operations}.

Remark 15
.

To determine the number of operations in Eq. (
61a
), we note that the summation over
j=\{0,1,\ldots,P\}
involves
P+1
multiplications and
P
additions.
After the summation, the result is multiplied by
-1
, adding one more multiplication.
Therefore, a total of
2(P+1)
arithmetic operations are required to compute
\hat{F}^{{{ui}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49315pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}
.
The same procedure was used to obtain the number of operations for the remaining components of
\hat{\mathbf{F}}
, and it is likewise used to compute those for
\hat{\mathbf{H}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{f}}
.

From Remark
10
, we observe that in
\hat{\mathbf{F}}
:
\hat{F}^{{{ui}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49315pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}
represents
R(P+1)^{2}
entries,
\hat{F}^{{{u}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}
represents
R^{2}(P+1)^{2}
entries,
\hat{F}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}
represents
(P+1)^{2}
entries, and
\hat{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}
represents
R(P+1)^{2}
entries.
Taking this observation into account, we obtain the total number of operations required to construct the matrix
\hat{\mathbf{F}}
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{no}(\hat{\mathbf{F}})

\displaystyle=R(P+1)^{2}\cdot 2(P+1)+R^{2}(P+1)^{2}\cdot 2(P+2)+(P+1)^{2}\cdot 0+R(P+1)^{2}\cdot 2R

(62)

\displaystyle=2R(P+1)^{2}\big[(R+1)(P+1)+2R\big].

Hence, the time complexity of matrix
\hat{\mathbf{F}}
is
\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{F}})=\mathcal{O}(R^{2}P^{3})
.

The time complexity of inverting matrix
\hat{\mathbf{F}}
using Gauss-Jordan elimination is
\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{F}})=\mathcal{O}(Q^{3})
, which means that the time complexity of matrix
\hat{\mathbf{X}}
is
\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{X}})=\mathcal{O}(R^{3}P^{3})
.

Next, we compute the time complexities of
\hat{\mathbf{H}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{L}}
(
=\hat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}
).
From Eq. (
37
),

(63a)

\hat{H}^{{{ui}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49315pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}=(-1)\sum_{j=0}^{P}I^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.24797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.24797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0699pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.62135pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta j}}}\!\bigg[\hat{\phi}^{uj}+(-\tfrac{1}{2})\bigg(\sum_{\gamma=1}^{R}\sum_{\rho=0}^{P}\hat{X}^{{{u}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}\hat{g}^{\gamma\rho}+\sum_{\rho=0}^{P}\hat{X}^{{{uj}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.44162pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.44162pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.10338pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.64526pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}\hat{h}^{\rho}\bigg)\bigg]\\
\quad\text{implies}\quad 2(R+2)(P+1)+1\text{ operations}.

(63b)

\hat{H}^{{{u}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}=K^{{{u}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}+(-1)\delta^{u}_{\alpha}\sum_{j=0}^{P}I^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.24797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.24797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0699pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.62135pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta j}}}\!\bigg[\hat{\lambda}^{j}+(-\tfrac{1}{2})\bigg(\sum_{\gamma=1}^{R}\sum_{\rho=0}^{P}\hat{X}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\gamma}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}\hat{g}^{\gamma\rho}+\sum_{\rho=0}^{P}\hat{X}^{{{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}\hat{h}^{\rho}\bigg)\bigg]\\
\quad\text{implies}\quad 2(R+2)(P+1)+3\text{ operations}.

(63c)

\hat{H}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}=0\quad\text{implies}\quad 0\text{ operations}.

(63d)

\hat{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}=(-2)\sum_{u=1}^{R}\eta_{\alpha u}\hat{H}^{{{ui}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49315pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}\quad\text{implies}\quad 2R\text{ operations}.

Thus, the total number of operations required to construct the matrix
\hat{\mathbf{H}}
, after recognizing that its components (see Remark
11
) share the same structure as those of
\hat{\mathbf{F}}
, is:

(64)

\mathrm{no}(\hat{\mathbf{H}})=R(P+1)^{2}\big[2(R+2)(R+1)(P+1)+5R+1\big],

meaning that the time complexity of
\hat{\mathbf{H}}
is
\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{H}})=\mathcal{O}(R^{3}P^{3})
and that of
\hat{\mathbf{L}}
(using again Gauss-Jordan elimination as the method for matrix inversion) is
\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{L}})=\mathcal{O}(R^{3}P^{3})
.

Lastly, we compute the time complexity of
\hat{\mathbf{f}}
.
From Eq. (
18
), we note that:

(65a)

\hat{g}^{ui}=\sum_{v=1}^{R}\sum_{j=0}^{P}K^{{{u}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.25119pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.25119pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55093pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{v}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}\hat{\phi}^{vj}+(-1)\sum_{j=0}^{P}\sum_{k=0}^{P}I^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jk}}}\hat{\lambda}^{k}\hat{\phi}^{uj}\quad\text{implies}\quad 2(P+1)(R+P+1)\text{ operations}.

(65b)

\hat{h}^{i}=(-1)I^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.97226pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.97226pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.9pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{00}}}+\sum_{u=1}^{R}\sum_{v=1}^{R}\sum_{j=0}^{P}\sum_{k=0}^{P}I^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jk}}}\eta_{uv}\hat{\phi}^{uj}\hat{\phi}^{vk}\quad\text{implies}\quad 2R^{2}(P+1)^{2}+1\text{ operations}.

From Remark
13
, we see that in
\hat{\mathbf{f}}
:
\hat{g}^{ui}
represents
R(P+1)
entries, and
\hat{h}^{i}
represents
P+1
entries.
Hence, the total number of operations required to construct the vector
\hat{\mathbf{f}}
is:

\displaystyle\mathrm{no}(\hat{\mathbf{f}})

\displaystyle=R(P+1)\cdot 2(P+1)(R+P+1)+(P+1)\cdot(2R^{2}(P+1)^{2}+1)

(66)

\displaystyle=(P+1)\big[2R(R+1)(P+1)^{2}+2R^{2}(P+1)+1\big],

which means that the time complexity of
\hat{\mathbf{f}}
is
\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{f}})=\mathcal{O}(R^{2}P^{3})
.

As a result, in Halley’s method, the time complexity of calculating
\mathbf{x}
(the next trial solution) is:

\displaystyle\mathrm{tc}\text{-Halley}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{F}})+\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{X}})+\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{H}})+\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{L}})+\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{f}})+\mathrm{tc}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(R^{2}P^{3})+\mathcal{O}(R^{3}P^{3})+\mathcal{O}(R^{3}P^{3})+\mathcal{O}(R^{3}P^{3})+\mathcal{O}(R^{2}P^{3})+\mathcal{O}(R^{2}P^{2})

(67)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(R^{3}P^{3}).

Interestingly, this is the same time complexity produced by Newton’s method (with
\hat{\mathbf{L}}=\hat{\mathbf{X}}
in Eq. (
59
)):

\displaystyle\mathrm{tc}\text{-Newton}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{F}})+\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{X}})+\mathrm{tc}(\hat{\mathbf{f}})+\mathrm{tc}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(R^{2}P^{3})+\mathcal{O}(R^{3}P^{3})+\mathcal{O}(R^{2}P^{3})+\mathcal{O}(R^{2}P^{2})

(68)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(R^{3}P^{3}).

However, it is important to add that the primary computational bottleneck in Newton’s method solely arises from computing
\hat{\mathbf{X}}
, whereas in Halley’s method, it stems from computing
\hat{\mathbf{X}}
,
\hat{\mathbf{H}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{L}}
.
As a result, a single iteration of Halley’s method is approximately three times more computationally expensive than one iteration of Newton’s method.
Nevertheless, as demonstrated in the previous section, Halley’s method offers a significant advantage in convergence rate, and the potential to further accelerate the solution process when the eigenvector components are roughly known.

8.
Illustrative example

Consider the problem of solving the 3-by-3 system of stochastic linear ODEs

(69)

\dot{\mathbf{x}}=\mathbf{K}\mathbf{x}\qquad:\Leftrightarrow\qquad\begin{bmatrix}\dot{x}\\
\dot{y}\\
\dot{z}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}2b&-b&0\\
-b&b+c&-c\\
0&-c&c\end{bmatrix}\!\begin{bmatrix}x\\
y\\
z\end{bmatrix},

where
b,c:\mathfrak{Z}\to\mathbb{R}
are random variables given by

b(\zeta)=5\zeta^{1}+20\quad\text{and}\quad c(\zeta)=10\zeta^{2}+15

(with the superscripts not denoting exponentiation), and
\zeta=(\zeta^{1},\zeta^{2})\sim\mathrm{Uniform}^{\otimes 2}
is a two-tuple random variable uniformly distributed in
\mathfrak{Z}=[-1,1]^{2}
, where the probability density function,
f:\mathfrak{Z}\to\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}
, is naturally given by
f(\zeta)=\tfrac{1}{4}
.
As a result, the measure on
(\mathfrak{Z},\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{Z}})
is given by:

\mu(\mathfrak{A})=\int_{\mathfrak{A}}f(\zeta)\,\mathrm{d}\zeta=\tfrac{1}{4}\int_{\mathfrak{A}}\mathrm{d}\zeta,\quad\text{for all $\mathfrak{A}\in\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{Z}}$}.

Since one solution of Eq. (
69
) is
\mathbf{x}=\boldsymbol{\upphi}e^{\lambda t}
, the problem reduces to solving Eq. (
1
), after noticing that
\mathbf{K}
is a random, real symmetric matrix of finite size (
R=3
).
For convenience,
\mathbf{K}:\mathfrak{Z}\to\mathrm{Sym}(3,\mathbb{R})
is taken hereafter as

(70)

\mathbf{K}(\zeta)=b(\zeta)\,\mathbf{B}+c(\zeta)\,\mathbf{C},

where

\mathbf{B}=\begin{bmatrix}2&-1&0\\
-1&1&0\\
0&0&0\end{bmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{C}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&1&-1\\
0&-1&1\end{bmatrix}.

Section
3
requires that the random basis is orthogonal with respect to
\mu\sim\mathrm{Uniform}^{\otimes 2}
.
One choice for such a random basis is to consider the tensor product of all Legendre polynomials.
In this example, six random basis functions are utilized to define the discretized version of
{Z}
, namely
{Z}^{[5]}=\mathrm{span}(\varphi_{j})_{j=0}^{5}
(
P=5
), where

\displaystyle\varphi_{0}(\zeta)=1,\quad\varphi_{1}(\zeta)=\zeta^{1},\quad\varphi_{2}(\zeta)=\zeta^{2},

\displaystyle\varphi_{3}(\zeta)=\tfrac{3}{2}(\zeta^{1})^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2},\quad\varphi_{4}(\zeta)=\zeta^{1}\zeta^{2},\quad\varphi_{5}(\zeta)=\tfrac{3}{2}(\zeta^{2})^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}.

By choice, the metric tensor endowed on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
is the Euclidean metric tensor, which means that
\eta_{uv}=\delta_{uv}
(the Kronecker delta).

Substituting Eq. (
70
) into Eq. (
10
a) gives the components
K^{{{u}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.25119pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.25119pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55093pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{v}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}
in matrix form:

(71)

\mathbf{K}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}=\frac{\langle\varphi_{i},\mathbf{K}\varphi_{j}\rangle}{\langle\varphi_{i},\varphi_{i}\rangle}=b^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}\mathbf{B}+c^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}\mathbf{C},

where
i,j\in\{0,1,\ldots,5\}
, and

(72)

b^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}=\frac{\langle\varphi_{i},b\varphi_{j}\rangle}{\langle\varphi_{i},\varphi_{i}\rangle}\quad\text{and}\quad c^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}=\frac{\langle\varphi_{i},c\varphi_{j}\rangle}{\langle\varphi_{i},\varphi_{i}\rangle}.

The task now reduces to determining the components
b^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}
,
c^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}
and
I^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jk}}}
using Eqs. (
72
) and (
10
b).
This is easy to do and is left to the reader to accomplish.

The entries of
\hat{\mathbf{F}}\in\mathrm{GL}(24,\mathbb{R})
are determined with Eq. (
33
) and then mapped using the correspondence set forth in Remark
10
.
For example, for
u=1
,
i=2
,
\alpha=2
and
\beta=3
, we have

\hat{F}^{{{ui}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.55734pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49315pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}=\hat{F}^{{{12}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.97226pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.97226pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.9pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{3}}}=-\sum_{j=0}^{5}I^{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69969pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69969pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.39166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{3j}}}\hat{\phi}^{1j}\mapsto\text{$(7,4)$-th entry of $\hat{\mathbf{F}}$},

\hat{F}^{{{u}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}=\hat{F}^{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{3}}}=K^{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{3}}}-\delta^{1}_{2}\sum_{j=0}^{5}I^{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69969pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69969pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.39166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{3j}}}\hat{\lambda}^{j}\mapsto\text{$(7,17)$-th entry of $\hat{\mathbf{F}}$},

\hat{F}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}=\hat{F}^{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{3}}}=0\mapsto\text{$(21,4)$-th entry of $\hat{\mathbf{F}}$},

\hat{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}=\hat{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{3}}}=-2\sum_{u=1}^{3}\sum_{j=0}^{5}I^{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69969pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69969pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.39166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{3j}}}\delta_{2u}\hat{\phi}^{uj}\mapsto\text{$(21,17)$-th entry of $\hat{\mathbf{F}}$},

where
\delta^{1}_{2}=\delta_{21}=\delta_{23}=0
and
\delta_{22}=1
.

Following a similar approach, the entries of
\hat{\mathbf{H}}\in\mathrm{GL}(24,\mathbb{R})
are determined with Eq. (
39
) and then mapped using the correspondence set forth in Remark
11
.
Alternatively,
\hat{\mathbf{H}}
can be computed with Eq. (
33
) provided that
\hat{x}
is updated with
\bar{x}
(see Remark
14
).
The entries of
\hat{\mathbf{X}}\in\mathrm{GL}(24,\mathbb{R})
are determined with Eq. (
34
).
The entries of
\bar{\mathbf{x}},\hat{\mathbf{x}},\hat{\mathbf{f}}\in\mathbb{R}^{24}
are obtained with Eqs. (
40
) and (
41
).

The algorithm outlined below provides the numerical solution to Eq. (
69
).

(1)

Initial guess:
Choose an initial guess
\hat{\mathbf{x}}
close to the expected solution.
This can be achieved by conducting a Monte Carlo simulation with a few number of realizations to position the initial guess sufficiently close to the solution.

(2)

Iteration:
Compute
\hat{\mathbf{F}}
,
\hat{\mathbf{X}}
,
\hat{\mathbf{H}}
,
\hat{\mathbf{L}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{f}}
.
Then, update the guess using:

\mathbf{x}=\hat{\mathbf{x}}-\hat{\mathbf{L}}\hat{\mathbf{f}}.

This follows from Eq. (
30
) with
\hat{\mathbf{L}}=\hat{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}
.
(We remark that for Newton’s method,
\hat{\mathbf{L}}
is taken as
\hat{\mathbf{X}}
.)

(3)

Convergence check:
Repeat step 2 until the change in magnitude of
\mathbf{x}
is smaller than a pre-specified tolerance
\epsilon
(that is,
\lVert\hat{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbf{x}\rVert_{2}\equiv\lVert\hat{\mathbf{L}}\hat{\mathbf{f}}\rVert_{2}<\epsilon
), or until the magnitude of
\hat{\mathbf{f}}
is smaller than
\epsilon
(that is,
\lVert\hat{\mathbf{f}}\rVert_{2}\equiv\lVert\hat{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbf{x})\rVert_{2}<\epsilon
).
In this work, we use the latter check, where
\lVert\,\cdot\,\rVert_{2}
denotes the Euclidean norm on
\mathbb{R}^{Q}
(
Q=24
for
R=3
and
P=5
).

Implementing the algorithm with
\epsilon=10^{-12}
and
P=5
yields

\mathbf{E}[\lambda]=\begin{cases}3.73451918626129&\text{for mode 1}\\
25.4620874810967&\text{for mode 2}\\
61.0547534054944&\text{for mode 3}\end{cases}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{Var}[\lambda]=\begin{cases}0.28824486249498&\text{for mode 1}\\
38.3590753966161&\text{for mode 2}\\
70.6920253818825&\text{for mode 3},\end{cases}

which follow from using Eqs. (
15
) and (
16
), respectively.

Fig.
1
compares the convergence of Newton’s method and Halley’s
method.
It is observed that Halley’s method not only exhibits a faster convergence rate than Newton’s method, but also converges in cases where Newton’s method may not, highlighting the robustness and efficiency of Halley’s approach.

Figure 1.
Convergence of Halley’s method and Newton’s method

9.
A case study

To demonstrate our method with a numerical example, we consider a 9-story office building located in a hurricane-prone region.
The building (Fig.
2
) is designed using a combination of frame elements and stiffening devices to enhance its dynamic performance.
The primary goal of this design is to assess whether the incorporation of stiffening devices along the height of the building can effectively improve the structural integrity of the building, increase occupant comfort, and distribute wind loads more evenly throughout the structure.

Figure 2.
Nine-story building under study

A linearized version of the differential equation governing the motion of the 9-story building, without including damping effects or applied forces, is:

(73)

\mathbf{M}\ddot{\mathbf{u}}+\mathbf{R}\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{0},

where
\mathbf{R}
is the stiffness matrix,
\mathbf{M}
is the mass matrix,
\mathbf{u}
is the displacement vector,
\ddot{\mathbf{u}}
is the acceleration vector, and
\mathbf{0}
is the zero vector.

For the purposes of this example, we consider nine translational (horizontal) degrees of freedom, one for each story, and take the mass matrix as
\mathbf{M}=m\mathbf{I}
, where
m
is the inter-story mass of the building in units of Mg, and
\mathbf{I}
is the 9-by-9 identity matrix.
As for the stiffness matrix, we take it as

(74)

\mathbf{R}(\zeta)=\mathbf{A}+b(\zeta)\,\mathbf{B}+c(\zeta)\,\mathbf{C},

where
b,c
are real-valued, uniformly-distributed random variables in units of kN/m, representing the lateral stiffnesses provided by the lower-level and upper-level stiffening devices, respectively, and
\mathbf{R},\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B},\mathbf{C}
are 9-by-9 real symmetric matrices with the entries of
\mathbf{R}
and
\mathbf{A}
in units of kN/m and the entries of
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{C}
unitless.
Specifically,
b(\zeta)=(50+7.5\zeta^{1})\times 10^{3}
,
c(\zeta)=(25+3.75\zeta^{2})\times 10^{3}
,
\zeta=(\zeta^{1},\zeta^{2})\sim\mathrm{Uniform}^{\otimes 2}
, and the random domain is
\mathfrak{Z}=[-1,1]^{2}
.
The nonzero lower-triangular entries of
\mathbf{A}
are:

\displaystyle A^{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{1}}}=a_{1}+a_{2},\quad A^{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{2}}}=A^{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{3}}}=A^{{{4}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{4}}}=2a_{2},\quad A^{{{5}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{5}}}=a_{2}+a_{3},\quad A^{{{6}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{6}}}=A^{{{7}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{7}}}=A^{{{8}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{8}}}=2a_{3},\quad A^{{{9}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{9}}}=a_{3},

\displaystyle A^{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{1}}}=A^{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{2}}}=A^{{{4}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{3}}}=A^{{{5}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{4}}}=-a_{2},\quad A^{{{6}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{5}}}=A^{{{7}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{6}}}=A^{{{8}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{7}}}=A^{{{9}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{8}}}=-a_{3},

where
a_{1}=170\times 10^{3}
,
a_{2}=120\times 10^{3}
and
a_{3}=75\times 10^{3}
represent the lateral stiffnesses of the first story, the second-to-fifth story, and the sixth-to-ninth story, respectively.
These stiffnesses are expressed in units of kN/m.
The nonzero lower-triangular entries of
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{C}
are:

\displaystyle B^{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{1}}}=B^{{{5}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{5}}}=C^{{{5}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{5}}}=C^{{{9}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{9}}}=1,\quad B^{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{3}}}=C^{{{7}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{7}}}=2,

\displaystyle B^{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{1}}}=B^{{{5}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{3}}}=C^{{{7}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{5}}}=C^{{{9}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{7}}}=-1.

If the mean values of
b
and
c
are used and
m=100
Mg, the fundamental period of the building without the stiffening devices is 1.12 s, whereas with the stiffening devices it is 0.88 s, indicating a significant reduction of more than 20% in the fundamental period of the building when the stiffening devices are employed.

We now solve the stochastic eigenvalue problem stated in Eq. (
1
) by setting
\mathbf{K}=\mathbf{R}
.
Thus, the eigenvalues are expressed in units of
\mathrm{Mg}/\mathrm{s}^{2}
, and the period of vibration of the building is computed with the expression:
T=2\pi\sqrt{m/\lambda}
.
The solution is numerically obtained using
P=5
and
\epsilon=10^{-8}
.
For reference, the nonrandom eigenvalues are listed in the second column of Table
1
using the mean values of
b
and
c
; that is,
b=50\times 10^{3}
and
c=25\times 10^{3}
(both in kN/m).

The solution outline is as follows.
First, six Monte Carlo simulations are employed to estimate the chaos coefficients.
The coefficients are computed using different sample sizes to establish the number of realizations needed to get a reliable estimate.
Fig.
3
illustrates the variance convergence of Monte Carlo simulations as a function of the sample size for the system’s odd modes.
When
N
is relatively small, significant deviations from the true variance (Monte Carlo simulation with
N=10^{7}
realizations) are observed, as expected.
However, as
N
increases, the variance estimates progressively converge to the true value thanks to the law of large numbers.
This is clearly illustrated in the figure, where the computed variances become more tightly concentrated around the true value for large
N
.

Figure 3.
Variance convergence of Monte Carlo simulations for the 1st, 3rd, 5th, 7th and 9th modes (variances are normalized relative to the variance obtained from
N=10^{7}
realizations)

Fig.
4
shows the relationship between the first, second and ninth eigenvalues and the random domain using Halley’s method.
The random domain is represented by a square region in the
\zeta^{1}\zeta^{2}
-plane, ranging from
-1
to
1
in both directions, while the eigenvalues are represented by contour lines.
This visualization is essential for understanding how the eigenvalues respond to variations in the random inputs.
The results show that an increase in the values of
\zeta^{1}
and
\zeta^{2}
leads to a gradual increase in
\lambda
, specifically for the first-to-fourth and seventh modes.
In contrast, for the eighth and ninth modes,
\lambda
increases primarily as a function of
\zeta^{1}
, while for the fifth and sixth modes, it increases mainly as a function of
\zeta^{2}
.
These findings provide valuable information on the stability and sensitivity of the system over the random domain.

Figure 4.
Plots of the 1st, 2nd and 9th eigenvalues as functions of
\zeta=(\zeta^{1},\zeta^{2})
using Halley’s method

Fig.
5
illustrates the comparative performance of Newton’s and Halley’s methods as a function of the iteration number.
In this context, iteration number zero represents the initial errors before the iterative scheme begins.
The dashed line indicates the pre-specified tolerance of
\epsilon=10^{-8}
.
The results demonstrate that Halley’s method consistently converges faster than Newton’s method.
Specifically, Halley’s method achieves the tolerance level in fewer iterations, with
some modes converging in as few as 4 iterations.
This efficiency suggests that the higher-order correction terms of Halley’s method offer a significant advantage in rapidly reducing errors.
In contrast, Newton’s method, while still converging effectively for most of the modes, requires more iterations to achieve the same level of tolerance.

Figure 5.
Convergence of Halley’s method and Newton’s method for the 1st, 3rd, 5th, 7th and 9th modes

Finally, Table
1
presents the results of a comparative performance analysis based on a Monte Carlo simulation with
N=10^{6}
realizations, and Newton’s and Halley’s method with 100 iterations allowed.
The table shows that, while all three methods provide comparable eigenvalue estimates, Halley’s method consistently achieved the more reliable estimates among the three methods.
In particular, it is noted that for the seventh mode, Newton’s method fails to converge within the 100 iterations allotted, while for the ninth mode, important discrepancies in the mean and variance values are obtained after Newton’s method identified an alternative, though still incorrect, solution.

10.
Conclusion

We have presented a novel numerical scheme to solve the stochastic eigenproblem via the spectral-chaos approach, using Halley’s method as the root-finding algorithm for the resulting set of deterministic quadratic equations displayed in Eq. (
9
).
Halley’s method was chosen not only for its cubic convergence properties but also because a higher-order Householder method would not provide additional improvements in the convergence rate, given the quadratic nature of the resulting system of equations.
Our method clearly marks a substantial improvement over the approach of Ghanem and Ghosh
[
21
]
, because it achieves a higher polynomial order of convergence and has the ability to accelerate the convergence rate when the eigenvector components are nearly known.
Moreover, to make the solution tractable, the stochastic eigenvalue problem was formulated in tensor notation to significantly reduce the complexity of the solution.
To the best of the authors’ knowledge, this approach had not been explored before as a compelling alternative to solving the stochastic eigenvalue problem.

Both the illustrative example and the case study demonstrated cases where Halley’s method converged while Newton’s method did not.
The comparative performance analyses revealed that Halley’s method converges faster and more reliably than Newton’s method.
Additionally, Halley’s method consistently delivered the most accurate results among the three methods.
Hence, Halley’s method is recommended for applications that require higher reliability and accuracy.

The algorithmic difference between Newton’s method and Halley’s method lies in the need to compute two extra objects for Halley’s case:
\hat{\mathbf{H}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{L}}
.
This additional computation increases the computational cost of Halley’s method compared to Newton’s method, resulting in a slower execution in each iteration.
However, the potential slowdown of Halley’s method can be offset by its fast convergence rate, especially in cases where Newton’s method struggles to find the solution due to an inadequate initial guess.
In Section
6
, it was shown that Halley’s method has the ability to accelerate the solution process when the eigenvector components are nearly known, an advantage not shared by Newton’s method.
Section
7
demonstrated that both Halley’s and Newton’s methods exhibit the same cubic asymptotic time complexity, indicating thereby that Halley’s method scales just as efficiently as Newton’s method in both high-dimensional spatial and random function spaces.

The authors believe, nonetheless, that further efforts are necessary to develop a numerical scheme that effectively aids in solving the stochastic eigenvalue problem with greater efficiency.
This can be accomplished by integrating advanced techniques from the current literature into Newton-based methods or by exploring innovative new approaches.

.

(75e)

\frac{\partial F^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\partial g^{\gamma\rho}}\bigg|_{f(\hat{x})}=\frac{\partial F^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\partial h^{\rho}}\bigg|_{f(\hat{x})}=0.

(75f)

\frac{\partial F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\partial Appendix B

In this section, we derive the derivative of
F
with respect to
x=(\lambda,\phi)
:

(
54
)

\frac{\partial^{2}f^{s}}{\partial x^{a}\partial x^{b}}\bigg|_{y}\equiv\frac{\partial F^{{{s}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}}{\partial x^{a}}\bigg|_{y}.

Similarly to Appendix A, this expression yields eight components.
The indices satisfy

,

and

(76a)

F^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\partial\lambda^{\rho}}\bigg|_{y}=\frac{\partial (76b)

\frac{\partial F^{{{u}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.72806pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80504pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.0036pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}}{\partial\lambda^{\rho}}\bigg|_{y}=-I^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.24797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.24797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0699pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.62135pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta Appendix C

In this section, we derive the individual components of the composite object:

(
55
)

\check{D}^{{{n}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{abc}}}:=\frac{\partial^{2}f^{s}}{\partial x^{a}\partial x^{b}}\bigg|_{y}\frac{\partial}{\partial f^{s}}\bigg(\frac{\partial f^{n}}{\partial x^{c}}\bigg)\bigg|_{f(y)}.

The indices satisfy
n\in(vj,j)
,
c\in(\sigma,\omega\sigma)
,
b\in(\beta,\alpha\beta)
,
a\in(\rho,\gamma\rho)
and
s\in(ui,i)
, where
u,v,w,\alpha,\gamma,\omega\in\{1,2,\ldots,R\}
and
i,j,k,\beta,\rho,\sigma\in\{0,1,\ldots,P\}
.

From Appendices A and B, it should be clear that:

(77a)

\check{D}^{{{(vj)}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.31734pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.31734pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.10486pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78917pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.21478pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.21478pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.66037pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.18599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\beta\sigma}}}=\check{D}^{{{(vj)}\mathchoice{\makebox[24.60513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[24.60513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.96597pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.68999pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.21478pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.21478pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.66037pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.18599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\beta(\omega\sigma)}}}=\check{D}^{{{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.31734pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.31734pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.10486pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78917pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\beta\sigma}}}=\check{D}^{{{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[23.76788pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[23.76788pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.45282pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.32346pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{(\gamma\rho)\beta\sigma}}}=\check{D}^{{{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[24.76613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[24.76613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[15.05049pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.75035pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho(\alpha\beta)\sigma}}}=\check{D}^{{{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[35.21667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[35.21667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[21.39845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[15.28464pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{(\gamma\rho)(\alpha\beta)\sigma}}}=\check{D}^{{{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[24.60513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[24.60513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.96597pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.68999pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\beta(\omega\sigma)}}}=0.

The other components of
\check{D}^{{{n}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{abc}}}
are as follows:

\displaystyle\check{D}^{{{(vj)}\mathchoice{\makebox[23.76788pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[23.76788pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.45282pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.32346pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.21478pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.21478pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.66037pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.18599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{(\gamma\rho)\beta\sigma}}}
